# Leyforge Forge Creator Guides

> Generated from `ForgeSectionGuides`. Edit the guide definitions, not this file.

Each workflow has a Basic checklist and an In-depth walkthrough. In-depth steps explain the exact control, example input, reason, expected result, verification, mistakes and recovery.

## Forge Home

### Basic

#### 1. Step 1: Start here when you are unsure what to open.

Where you are: Forge Home / New source bar
What to click: Source type
What to select or type: Choose the source type named in the step (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Start here when you are unsure what to open. Forge changes how an asset looks; it does not change recipes, inventory rules, machine logic or saved-world identity.
Why now: This establishes the source and goal before dependent work begins.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The requested source type is visible and its relevant editor becomes available.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 2. Step 2: Open Asset Browser to edit an existing presentation, or New Asset Wizard to create the first Forge source for a gameplay ID.

Where you are: Forge Home / New source bar
What to click: Name
What to select or type: Training Example (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Open Asset Browser to edit an existing presentation, or New Asset Wizard to create the first Forge source for a gameplay ID.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 3. Step 3: Look at the four totals: indexed presentations are all known assets, approved sources are live Forge replacements, drafts are work in progress, and legacy fallbacks still use older visuals.

Where you are: Forge Home / New source bar
What to click: Stable slug
What to select or type: training_example (Lowercase letters, numbers and underscores; do not include the automatic domain prefix.)
Action: Look at the four totals: indexed presentations are all known assets, approved sources are live Forge replacements, drafts are work in progress, and legacy fallbacks still use older visuals.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 4. Step 4: After opening an asset, use Block Surface for a cube made from six textures, Voxel Model for a sculpted 3D shape, or Compound & Sockets for machines with separate moving parts.

Where you are: Forge Home / New source bar
What to click: Create new source
What to select or type: Press the button after Name and Stable slug are valid (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: After opening an asset, use Block Surface for a cube made from six textures, Voxel Model for a sculpted 3D shape, or Compound & Sockets for machines with separate moving parts.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 5. Step 5: Watch Live Preview while editing.

Where you are: Forge Home / source editor
What to click: Preview output
What to select or type: Use the concrete value stated in the action (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Watch Live Preview while editing. Drag to orbit, Shift-drag or right/middle-drag to pan, scroll to zoom, and press Reset if the model leaves the frame.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 6. Step 6: Press Save Draft regularly or Ctrl+S.

Where you are: Forge Home / source editor
What to click: Save Draft
What to select or type: Press Validate; no text entry (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Press Save Draft regularly or Ctrl+S. Draft saves are recoverable and do not replace the approved in-game presentation.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 7. Step 7: Press Validate or Ctrl+Enter before baking.

Where you are: Forge Home / source editor
What to click: Validate
What to select or type: Compare the preview with the expected result (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Press Validate or Ctrl+Enter before baking. Fix red errors first; read warnings to decide whether the result is intentional.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 8. Step 8: Use Bake Preview for a temporary test.

Where you are: Forge Home / source editor
What to click: Preview output
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Use Bake Preview for a temporary test. Use Approve & Bake only when the source is saved, validation has no blockers, and the model looks correct in preview and world tests.
Why now: This final check proves the earlier steps form a safe, usable result.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The finished capstone reopens with a receipt-matched preview while human publication approval remains explicit.
How to verify: Reopen the source, run Validate again and confirm the preview and stable ID match the finished capstone.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

## Library & Create

### Basic

#### 3. Step 3: Read Author and Inspect badges as capability labels.

What to click: Create new source
What to select or type: Press the button after Name and Stable slug are valid (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)

## Items & Blocks

### Basic

#### 1. Step 1: Open or create an asset first; asset-required workflows explain why they are disabled when nothing is open.

Where you are: Items & Blocks / New source bar
What to click: Source type
What to select or type: Choose the source type named in the step (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Open or create an asset first; asset-required workflows explain why they are disabled when nothing is open.
Why now: This establishes the source and goal before dependent work begins.

#### 2. Step 2: Choose Surface for full cubes, Voxel Model for sculpted forms, and Compound for multiple named moving parts.

Where you are: Items & Blocks / New source bar
What to click: Name
What to select or type: Workshop Lantern (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Choose Surface for full cubes, Voxel Model for sculpted forms, and Compound for multiple named moving parts.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.

#### 1. Step 1: Capstone: create an animated workshop lantern from a new draft.

Where you are: Items & Blocks / New source bar
What to click: Source type
What to select or type: Choose the source type named in the step (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Capstone: create an animated workshop lantern from a new draft. Click New Asset Wizard, select a small placeable gameplay identity, choose Compound, enter Workshop Lantern and workshop_lantern, then click Create or Open Project Source.
Why now: starting from a new linked draft protects the source already open and fixes the stable identity before any parts refer to it.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.

#### 2. Step 2: Open Voxel Model Editor for the body part.

Where you are: Items & Blocks / New source bar
What to click: Name
What to select or type: Workshop Lantern (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Open Voxel Model Editor for the body part. Select Y axis, slice 0, Pencil, radius 1 and palette role metal_dark; paint a 6 x 6 base, then add a narrower frame on higher slices.
Why now: the silhouette establishes occupied geometry before materials, collision or animation depend on its dimensions.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.

#### 3. Step 3: Open Palette & Materials.

Where you are: Items & Blocks / New source bar
What to click: Stable slug
What to select or type: workshop_lantern (Lowercase letters, numbers and underscores; do not include the automatic domain prefix.)
Action: Open Palette & Materials. Add metal_dark, glass_warm and flame_glow; bind them to metal, transparent glass and emissive Material DNA, then return to the voxel canvas and paint the window and flame.
Why now: semantic material roles let later variants and damage states change appearance without repainting geometry.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.

Where you are: Items & Blocks / New source bar
What to click: Create new source
What to select or type: Press the button after Name and Stable slug are valid (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Open Compound Parts & Sockets. Name the parts body, door and flame, place the door pivot on its hinge, and add light, VFX and audio sockets parented to flame.
Why now: animation and effects must target stable part and socket names rather than scene paths.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.

What to click: The property or canvas named in this step

Action: Open Collision & Placement Footprint. Choose Single 1 x 1, add a compact body collision box, include the anchor cell and allow four floor rotations.

Action: Open Animation, Effects & Runtime States. Add a door_open transform clip, a flame_pulse material clip and inactive, active and damaged bindings; include an explicit stop action for the light loop.
Why now: pivots and sockets are stable, so animation tracks and loop ownership can now resolve safely.

Action: Click Save Draft, then Validate. Correct empty voxels, missing Material DNA, collision or socket blockers; click Bake Preview and open World & Machine Test Preview with one and three instances.
Why now: validation and crowded world preview prove that modelling, materials, states, collision, animation and icon settings work together.

## VFX

### Basic

#### 1. Step 1: Open Live VFX Graph Editor and create a voxel Form, bounded Graph and top-level Effect.

Where you are: VFX / New source bar
What to click: Source type
What to select or type: Choose the source type named in the step (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Open Live VFX Graph Editor and create a voxel Form, bounded Graph and top-level Effect.
Why now: This establishes the source and goal before dependent work begins.
What this controls: This edits the visible form, bounded execution graph or assembly effect. Explicit limits prevent one effect from exhausting scene budgets.
Expected result: The requested source type is visible and its relevant editor becomes available.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Creating a graph cycle, omitting lifetime/spawn bounds, or approving before reduced-motion and dense previews.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.

## Audio

### Basic

#### 1. Step 1: Open Waveform & Event Editor and choose Synth Instrument / Sound to work without uploading a file.

Where you are: Audio / New source bar
What to click: Source type
What to select or type: Choose the source type named in the step (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Open Waveform & Event Editor and choose Synth Instrument / Sound to work without uploading a file.
Why now: This establishes the source and goal before dependent work begins.
What this controls: This edits a reproducible sound recipe, non-destructive source transform or managed Sound Event. Playback ownership and limits prevent stuck loops and excessive voices.
Expected result: The requested source type is visible and its relevant editor becomes available.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Clipping gain, an inaudible envelope, a loop without a stop path, missing caption, or too many simultaneous voices.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: source, spatial profile, runtime product, receipt, approval. Related: captions and critical-cue equivalents.

## Asset Browser

### Basic

## New Asset Wizard

### Basic

#### 1. Step 1: Open the Canonical gameplay presentation list and choose the existing block or item that should receive new visuals.

Where you are: New Asset Wizard / New source bar
What to click: Source type
What to select or type: Choose the source type named in the step (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Open the Canonical gameplay presentation list and choose the existing block or item that should receive new visuals. This prevents duplicate gameplay IDs.
Why now: This establishes the source and goal before dependent work begins.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The requested source type is visible and its relevant editor becomes available.

Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 2. Step 2: Choose Recommended from the registry when you want Forge to select Surface, Voxel or Compound from the known shape and create starter geometry.

Where you are: New Asset Wizard / New source bar
What to click: Name
What to select or type: Training Example (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Choose Recommended from the registry when you want Forge to select Surface, Voxel or Compound from the known shape and create starter geometry.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.

Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 3. Step 3: Choose Block Surface when the finished asset is a full cube whose shape stays cubic and only its six painted faces need to change.

Where you are: New Asset Wizard / New source bar
What to click: Stable slug
What to select or type: training_example (Lowercase letters, numbers and underscores; do not include the automatic domain prefix.)
Action: Choose Block Surface when the finished asset is a full cube whose shape stays cubic and only its six painted faces need to change.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.

How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 4. Step 4: Choose Voxel Model when you need a non-cube silhouette, holes, handles, blades, gears, cranks, furniture or any other sculpted 3D form.

Where you are: New Asset Wizard / New source bar
What to click: Create new source
What to select or type: Press the button after Name and Stable slug are valid (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Choose Voxel Model when you need a non-cube silhouette, holes, handles, blades, gears, cranks, furniture or any other sculpted 3D form. This option begins with a blank 32 x 32 x 32 volume.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.

Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 5. Step 5: Click Create or Open Project Source.

Where you are: New Asset Wizard / Source editor
What to click: The property or canvas named in this step
What to select or type: Use the concrete value stated in the action (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Click Create or Open Project Source. If the source already exists, Forge opens it and switches to the selected Surface or Voxel mode without deleting the inactive source.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.

How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 6. Step 6: In the chosen editor, add at least one visible colour or voxel.

Where you are: New Asset Wizard / source editor
What to click: Validate
What to select or type: Press Validate; no text entry (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: In the chosen editor, add at least one visible colour or voxel. A completely empty Voxel Model cannot validate or bake.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.

#### 7. Step 7: Open Palette & Materials to name colours by purpose, choose exact colours, and bind each role to Material DNA.

Where you are: New Asset Wizard / Preview output
What to click: Preview
What to select or type: Compare the preview with the expected result (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Open Palette & Materials to name colours by purpose, choose exact colours, and bind each role to Material DNA.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.

Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 8. Step 8: Open Collision & Placement and confirm how many world blocks the object occupies.

Where you are: New Asset Wizard / Document actions
What to click: Approve & Save
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Open Collision & Placement and confirm how many world blocks the object occupies. Doors normally use Tall 1 x 2; ordinary blocks use Single 1 x 1.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.

#### 9. Step 9: Save Draft, Validate, and then Bake Preview.

Where you are: New Asset Wizard / source editor
What to click: Save Draft
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Save Draft, Validate, and then Bake Preview. Do not Approve & Bake until the preview, collision and placement footprint are all correct.
Why now: This final check proves the earlier steps form a safe, usable result.

How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 3. Step 3: Choose Block Surface when the finished asset is a full cube whose shape stays cubic and only its six painted faces need to change.

Where you are: New Asset Wizard / New source bar
What to click: Stable slug
What to select or type: training_example (Lowercase letters, numbers and underscores; do not include the automatic domain prefix.)
Action: Choose Block Surface when the finished asset is a full cube whose shape stays cubic and only its six painted faces need to change.
Why now: This uses the output of the previous step and prepares the next one.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 4. Step 4: Choose Voxel Model when you need a non-cube silhouette, holes, handles, blades, gears, cranks, furniture or any other sculpted 3D form.

Where you are: New Asset Wizard / New source bar
What to click: Create new source
What to select or type: Press the button after Name and Stable slug are valid (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Choose Voxel Model when you need a non-cube silhouette, holes, handles, blades, gears, cranks, furniture or any other sculpted 3D form. This option begins with a blank 32 x 32 x 32 volume.
Why now: This uses the output of the previous step and prepares the next one.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 5. Step 5: Click Create or Open Project Source.

Where you are: New Asset Wizard / Source editor
What to click: The property or canvas named in this step
What to select or type: Use the concrete value stated in the action (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Click Create or Open Project Source. If the source already exists, Forge opens it and switches to the selected Surface or Voxel mode without deleting the inactive source.
Why now: This uses the output of the previous step and prepares the next one.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 6. Step 6: In the chosen editor, add at least one visible colour or voxel.

Where you are: New Asset Wizard / source editor
What to click: Validate
What to select or type: Press Validate; no text entry (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: In the chosen editor, add at least one visible colour or voxel. A completely empty Voxel Model cannot validate or bake.
Why now: This uses the output of the previous step and prepares the next one.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 7. Step 7: Open Palette & Materials to name colours by purpose, choose exact colours, and bind each role to Material DNA.

Where you are: New Asset Wizard / Preview output
What to click: Preview
What to select or type: Compare the preview with the expected result (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Open Palette & Materials to name colours by purpose, choose exact colours, and bind each role to Material DNA.
Why now: This uses the output of the previous step and prepares the next one.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 8. Step 8: Open Collision & Placement and confirm how many world blocks the object occupies.

Where you are: New Asset Wizard / Document actions
What to click: Approve & Save
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Open Collision & Placement and confirm how many world blocks the object occupies. Doors normally use Tall 1 x 2; ordinary blocks use Single 1 x 1.
Why now: This uses the output of the previous step and prepares the next one.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 9. Step 9: Save Draft, Validate, and then Bake Preview.

Where you are: New Asset Wizard / source editor
What to click: Save Draft
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Save Draft, Validate, and then Bake Preview. Do not Approve & Bake until the preview, collision and placement footprint are all correct.
Why now: This final check proves the earlier steps form a safe, usable result.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The finished capstone reopens with a receipt-matched preview while human publication approval remains explicit.
How to verify: Reopen the source, run Validate again and confirm the preview and stable ID match the finished capstone.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

## Block Surface Editor

### Basic

#### 1. Step 1: Use Block Surface only for a cube.

Where you are: Block Surface Editor / New source bar
What to click: Source type
What to select or type: Choose the source type named in the step (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Use Block Surface only for a cube. If you need to sculpt the silhouette, choose Voxel Model in Authoring mode and click Switch authoring mode; your surface work is kept for later.
Why now: This establishes the source and goal before dependent work begins.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The requested source type is visible and its relevant editor becomes available.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 2. Step 2: Choose Top, Bottom, North, South, East or West from Face.

Where you are: Block Surface Editor / New source bar
What to click: Name
What to select or type: Training Example (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Choose Top, Bottom, North, South, East or West from Face. The canvas always edits only the selected face.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 3. Step 3: Choose a Palette role.

Where you are: Block Surface Editor / New source bar
What to click: Stable slug
What to select or type: training_example (Lowercase letters, numbers and underscores; do not include the automatic domain prefix.)
Action: Choose a Palette role. The colour square beside each name shows exactly what the selected role paints. Edit or add roles under Palette & Materials.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 4. Step 4: Choose Pencil for single cells, Square/Circle/Diamond for larger stamps, or Fill bucket to replace one connected area.

Where you are: Block Surface Editor / New source bar
What to click: Create new source
What to select or type: Press the button after Name and Stable slug are valid (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Choose Pencil for single cells, Square/Circle/Diamond for larger stamps, or Fill bucket to replace one connected area. Shape radius controls how large the shape tools are.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 5. Step 5: Hold the left mouse button and drag to keep painting.

Where you are: Block Surface Editor / Source editor
What to click: The property or canvas named in this step
What to select or type: Use the concrete value stated in the action (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Hold the left mouse button and drag to keep painting. Hold the right mouse button and drag to restore the base role. Each continuous batch can be undone with Ctrl+Z.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 6. Step 6: Enable Colour randomizer and tick the palette roles to mix while painting.

Where you are: Block Surface Editor / Document actions
What to click: Validate
What to select or type: Press Validate; no text entry (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Enable Colour randomizer and tick the palette roles to mix while painting. Select at least two roles for visible variation such as stone speckle, wood grain or worn edges.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 7. Step 7: To reuse a finished material layout, enter a Pattern name and click Save current face pattern.

Where you are: Block Surface Editor / Preview output
What to click: Preview
What to select or type: Compare the preview with the expected result (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: To reuse a finished material layout, enter a Pattern name and click Save current face pattern. The pattern stores its colour roles and Material DNA, not only raw palette numbers.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 8. Step 8: Select a saved pattern and click Apply pattern to current face.

Where you are: Block Surface Editor / Document actions
What to click: Approve & Save
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Select a saved pattern and click Apply pattern to current face. The pattern tiles across the face, so it can be reused on blocks of the same or different texture resolution.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 9. Step 9: To duplicate a face, click Copy current face, tick every destination side, choose Copy, Mirror or Rotate, then click Paste to selected faces.

Where you are: Block Surface Editor / Document actions
What to click: Approve & Save
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: To duplicate a face, click Copy current face, tick every destination side, choose Copy, Mirror or Rotate, then click Paste to selected faces.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 10. Step 10: Use Mirror current or Rotate current when only the selected face is upside down or facing the wrong direction.

Where you are: Block Surface Editor / Document actions
What to click: Approve & Save
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Use Mirror current or Rotate current when only the selected face is upside down or facing the wrong direction. These actions do not replace the copied clipboard face.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 11. Step 11: Enable 2 x 2 tile preview to inspect repeating seams.

Where you are: Block Surface Editor / source editor
What to click: Preview output
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Enable 2 x 2 tile preview to inspect repeating seams. The tiled view is read-only; turn it off before painting again.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 12. Step 12: Inspect all six sides in Live Preview, Save Draft, and Validate before baking.

Where you are: Block Surface Editor / source editor
What to click: Save Draft
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Inspect all six sides in Live Preview, Save Draft, and Validate before baking. Pay special attention to top/bottom orientation and edge continuity.
Why now: This final check proves the earlier steps form a safe, usable result.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The finished capstone reopens with a receipt-matched preview while human publication approval remains explicit.

#### 9. Step 9: To duplicate a face, click Copy current face, tick every destination side, choose Copy, Mirror or Rotate, then click Paste to selected faces.

Where you are: Block Surface Editor / Document actions
What to click: Approve & Save
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)

#### 10. Step 10: Use Mirror current or Rotate current when only the selected face is upside down or facing the wrong direction.

Where you are: Block Surface Editor / Document actions
What to click: Approve & Save
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Use Mirror current or Rotate current when only the selected face is upside down or facing the wrong direction. These actions do not replace the copied clipboard face.
Why now: This uses the output of the previous step and prepares the next one.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.

#### 11. Step 11: Enable 2 x 2 tile preview to inspect repeating seams.

Where you are: Block Surface Editor / source editor
What to click: Preview output
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Enable 2 x 2 tile preview to inspect repeating seams. The tiled view is read-only; turn it off before painting again.
Why now: This uses the output of the previous step and prepares the next one.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.

How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 12. Step 12: Inspect all six sides in Live Preview, Save Draft, and Validate before baking.

Where you are: Block Surface Editor / source editor
What to click: Save Draft
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Inspect all six sides in Live Preview, Save Draft, and Validate before baking. Pay special attention to top/bottom orientation and edge continuity.
Why now: This final check proves the earlier steps form a safe, usable result.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The finished capstone reopens with a receipt-matched preview while human publication approval remains explicit.
How to verify: Reopen the source, run Validate again and confirm the preview and stable ID match the finished capstone.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

## Voxel Model Editor

### Basic

#### 1. Step 1: Use Voxel Model for a true 3D shape.

Where you are: Voxel Model Editor / New source bar
What to click: Source type
What to select or type: Choose the source type named in the step (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Use Voxel Model for a true 3D shape. If the asset currently uses Block Surface, choose Voxel Model in Authoring mode and click Switch authoring mode; the previous surface source remains available.
Why now: This establishes the source and goal before dependent work begins.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The requested source type is visible and its relevant editor becomes available.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 2. Step 2: Think of the model as a stack of flat layers.

Where you are: Voxel Model Editor / New source bar
What to click: Name
What to select or type: Workshop Lantern (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Think of the model as a stack of flat layers. Choose X to look from the side, Y to edit horizontal height layers, or Z to work from the front/back.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 3. Step 3: Move the slice slider to the layer you want.

Where you are: Voxel Model Editor / New source bar
What to click: Stable slug
What to select or type: workshop_lantern (Lowercase letters, numbers and underscores; do not include the automatic domain prefix.)
Action: Move the slice slider to the layer you want. The label shows the active axis and exact layer number; only that layer is painted.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 4. Step 4: Choose a Palette role.

Where you are: Voxel Model Editor / source editor
What to click: Preview output
What to select or type: Press the button after Name and Stable slug are valid (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Choose a Palette role. Its colour swatch previews the paint colour. Add or change roles and Material DNA under Palette & Materials.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 5. Step 5: Choose Pencil, Square, Circle, Diamond or Fill bucket, then choose Shape radius.

Where you are: Voxel Model Editor / Source editor
What to click: The property or canvas named in this step
What to select or type: Use the concrete value stated in the action (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Choose Pencil, Square, Circle, Diamond or Fill bucket, then choose Shape radius. Start with Pencil and radius 1 while learning.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 6. Step 6: Hold left-click and drag to add or recolour microvoxels.

Where you are: Voxel Model Editor / Document actions
What to click: Validate
What to select or type: Press Validate; no text entry (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Hold left-click and drag to add or recolour microvoxels. Hold right-click and drag to remove them. Empty dark cells contain no geometry.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 7. Step 7: Enable Mirror X for symmetrical objects.

Where you are: Voxel Model Editor / Preview output
What to click: Preview
What to select or type: Compare the preview with the expected result (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Enable Mirror X for symmetrical objects. The gold centre line shows the mirror plane and every stroke is applied to both sides in one undoable command.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.

How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 8. Step 8: Enable Colour randomizer and tick two or more palette roles to paint controlled colour variation across the model.

Where you are: Voxel Model Editor / Document actions
What to click: Approve & Save
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Enable Colour randomizer and tick two or more palette roles to paint controlled colour variation across the model.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.

Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 9. Step 9: Click Copy current layer, move the slider or change axis to a layer with matching dimensions, then click Paste onto current layer.

Where you are: Voxel Model Editor / Document actions
What to click: Approve & Save
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Click Copy current layer, move the slider or change axis to a layer with matching dimensions, then click Paste onto current layer. Paste replaces occupied and empty cells and can be undone once.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 10. Step 10: To reuse only a material layout, save the current layer as a named colour pattern.

Where you are: Voxel Model Editor / Document actions
What to click: Approve & Save
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: To reuse only a material layout, save the current layer as a named colour pattern. Applying that pattern recolours occupied voxels but keeps the destination object's shape and holes.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 11. Step 11: Orbit Live Preview frequently and inspect the model from X, Y and Z.

Where you are: Voxel Model Editor / source editor
What to click: Preview output
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Orbit Live Preview frequently and inspect the model from X, Y and Z. A good-looking front layer can still hide missing depth or accidental internal voxels.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.

Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 12. Step 12: Save Draft and Validate.

Where you are: Voxel Model Editor / source editor
What to click: Save Draft
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Save Draft and Validate. Resolve the empty-volume error, then use Bake Preview before approving the asset.
Why now: This final check proves the earlier steps form a safe, usable result.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.

What to click: Save Draft
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Save Draft and Validate. Resolve the empty-volume error, then use Bake Preview before approving the asset.
Why now: This final check proves the earlier steps form a safe, usable result.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The finished capstone reopens with a receipt-matched preview while human publication approval remains explicit.
How to verify: Reopen the source, run Validate again and confirm the preview and stable ID match the finished capstone.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

## Palette Roles & Material DNA

### Basic

#### 1. Step 1: A palette role is a named paint slot such as oak_base, oak_shadow, iron_edge or glow.

Where you are: Palette Roles & Material DNA / New source bar
What to click: Source type
What to select or type: Choose the source type named in the step (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: A palette role is a named paint slot such as oak_base, oak_shadow, iron_edge or glow. Models store the role number so you can change the colour later without repainting every cell.
Why now: This establishes the source and goal before dependent work begins.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The requested source type is visible and its relevant editor becomes available.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 2. Step 2: Use the colour square and colour picker in each row to set the visible albedo colour.

Where you are: Palette Roles & Material DNA / source editor
What to click: Preview output
What to select or type: Training Example (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Use the colour square and colour picker in each row to set the visible albedo colour. The small swatch updates immediately and Live Preview refreshes after the change.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.

Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 3. Step 3: Rename generic roles to describe their purpose.

Where you are: Palette Roles & Material DNA / New source bar
What to click: Stable slug
What to select or type: training_example (Lowercase letters, numbers and underscores; do not include the automatic domain prefix.)
Action: Rename generic roles to describe their purpose. Press Enter after typing the new role name so the change is applied.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.

Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 4. Step 4: Open the Material DNA list on each row and choose the physical/rendering family that belongs to that colour.

Where you are: Palette Roles & Material DNA / New source bar
What to click: Create new source
What to select or type: Press the button after Name and Stable slug are valid (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Open the Material DNA list on each row and choose the physical/rendering family that belongs to that colour. Existing material.generic.stone labels are shown as missing/custom until you choose a real library definition.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.

How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 5. Step 5: Material DNA stores shared rendering intent such as wood, metal, glass, stone or emissive magic.

Where you are: Palette Roles & Material DNA / Source editor
What to click: The property or canvas named in this step
What to select or type: Use the concrete value stated in the action (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Material DNA stores shared rendering intent such as wood, metal, glass, stone or emissive magic. It does not replace the visible palette colour.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.

#### 6. Step 6: Click Add palette role when the asset needs another paint colour.

Where you are: Palette Roles & Material DNA / Document actions
What to click: Validate
What to select or type: Press Validate; no text entry (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Click Add palette role when the asset needs another paint colour. New roles use an available library material instead of the old hard-coded generic stone reference.

#### 7. Step 7: To make a new Material DNA, enter a lowercase Material ID beginning with material., a readable display name and a family such as wood or metal.

Where you are: Palette Roles & Material DNA / Preview output
What to click: Preview
What to select or type: Compare the preview with the expected result (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: To make a new Material DNA, enter a lowercase Material ID beginning with material., a readable display name and a family such as wood or metal.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.

#### 8. Step 8: Choose the rendering class, then set roughness, metallic and emission.

Where you are: Palette Roles & Material DNA / Document actions
What to click: Approve & Save
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Choose the rendering class, then set roughness, metallic and emission. Opaque is normal; transparent and emissive should be used only when the asset needs them.

## Compound Parts & Sockets

### Basic

#### 1. Step 1: Use Compound authoring for machines made from independently positioned or animated pieces, such as a furnace body, door, gear and flame.

Where you are: Compound Parts & Sockets / New source bar
What to click: Source type
What to select or type: Choose the source type named in the step (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Use Compound authoring for machines made from independently positioned or animated pieces, such as a furnace body, door, gear and flame.
Why now: This establishes the source and goal before dependent work begins.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
