#### 2. Step 2: Review Named parts first.

Where you are: Compound Parts & Sockets / New source bar
What to click: Name
What to select or type: Training Example (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Review Named parts first. Each part key is a permanent animation target, so use simple stable names such as body, door, gear or flame.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.

#### 3. Step 3: Enter a new part key and click Add voxel part.

Where you are: Compound Parts & Sockets / New source bar
What to click: Stable slug
What to select or type: training_example (Lowercase letters, numbers and underscores; do not include the automatic domain prefix.)
Action: Enter a new part key and click Add voxel part. Open its voxel source through the available model workflow before relying on it in animation.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.

Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 4. Step 4: Set the local transform to place the part relative to the asset origin.

Where you are: Compound Parts & Sockets / New source bar
What to click: Create new source
What to select or type: Press the button after Name and Stable slug are valid (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Set the local transform to place the part relative to the asset origin. Keep the body near the origin so collision and placement remain predictable.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.

#### 5. Step 5: Place a pivot at the real hinge or axle before animating.

Where you are: Compound Parts & Sockets / Source editor
What to click: The property or canvas named in this step
What to select or type: Use the concrete value stated in the action (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Place a pivot at the real hinge or axle before animating. A door pivots at its edge; a gear pivots at its centre.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.

#### 6. Step 6: Mark only genuinely moving pieces as animated.

Where you are: Compound Parts & Sockets / Document actions
What to click: Validate
What to select or type: Press Validate; no text entry (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Mark only genuinely moving pieces as animated. Static pieces can remain combined for a cheaper runtime mesh.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.

#### 7. Step 7: Add typed sockets for flames, smoke, audio, held items, logistics or interactions, and parent them to the part they should follow.

Where you are: Compound Parts & Sockets / Preview output
What to click: Preview
What to select or type: Compare the preview with the expected result (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Add typed sockets for flames, smoke, audio, held items, logistics or interactions, and parent them to the part they should follow.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 8. Step 8: Validate against the presentation contract.

Where you are: Compound Parts & Sockets / source editor
What to click: Validate
What to select or type: Use only after validation and human review (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Validate against the presentation contract. Missing required parts or sockets are blockers because runtime effects and animations could not find their targets.
Why now: This final check proves the earlier steps form a safe, usable result.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The finished capstone reopens with a receipt-matched preview while human publication approval remains explicit.
How to verify: Reopen the source, run Validate again and confirm the preview and stable ID match the finished capstone.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.

## Collision & Placement Footprint

### Basic

#### 1. Step 1: Collision controls where players and other bodies can physically move.

Where you are: Collision & Placement Footprint / New source bar
What to click: Source type
What to select or type: Choose the source type named in the step (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Collision controls where players and other bodies can physically move. Placement footprint reserves world-grid cells so two objects cannot occupy the same space.
Why now: This establishes the source and goal before dependent work begins.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The requested source type is visible and its relevant editor becomes available.

Action: Start with the smallest simple collision boxes that cover the solid usable shape. Avoid filling intentional holes, doorways or handle gaps.

#### 5. Step 5: Set allowed rotations to match how the object may face in the world.

## Animation, Effects & Runtime States

### Basic

#### 1. Step 1: Create and name compound parts before animation.

Where you are: Animation, Effects & Runtime States / New source bar
What to click: Source type
What to select or type: Choose the source type named in the step (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Create and name compound parts before animation. Animation tracks target stable part keys; renaming a part later can break the clip.
Why now: This establishes the source and goal before dependent work begins.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The requested source type is visible and its relevant editor becomes available.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 2. Step 2: Use transform clips for doors, handles, gears and other rigid pieces.

Where you are: Animation, Effects & Runtime States / New source bar
What to click: Name
What to select or type: Training Example (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Use transform clips for doors, handles, gears and other rigid pieces. Put the pivot at the hinge or axle before adding rotation keys.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.

How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 3. Step 3: For a continuously turning gear, animate a full rotation and use a looping clip.

Where you are: Animation, Effects & Runtime States / New source bar
What to click: Stable slug
What to select or type: training_example (Lowercase letters, numbers and underscores; do not include the automatic domain prefix.)
Action: For a continuously turning gear, animate a full rotation and use a looping clip. If it swings and snaps back, check the pivot, rotation axis, final key and loop interpolation.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.

## Blueprint Designer Handoff

### Basic

#### 1. Step 1: Use this page when an asset will be placed by a settlement or structure blueprint rather than only by the player.

Where you are: Blueprint Designer Handoff / New source bar
What to click: Source type
What to select or type: Choose the source type named in the step (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Use this page when an asset will be placed by a settlement or structure blueprint rather than only by the player.
Why now: This establishes the source and goal before dependent work begins.
What this controls: This edits canonical structure geometry, semantic markers, typed connectors or bounded generation rules. These records control placement and presentation, not settlement gameplay truth.
Expected result: The requested source type is visible and its relevant editor becomes available.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Using unsaved module IDs, reversed connector directions, blocked clearance or an unbounded rule set.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: source, connector, bake, receipt, approval. Related: Items & Blocks material roles and Test & Delivery.

#### 2. Step 2: Confirm the stable presentation ID; blueprint data should reference the canonical ID rather than a generated scene path.

Where you are: Blueprint Designer Handoff / New source bar
What to click: Name
What to select or type: Training Guardhouse (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Confirm the stable presentation ID; blueprint data should reference the canonical ID rather than a generated scene path.

How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: source, connector, bake, receipt, approval. Related: Items & Blocks material roles and Test & Delivery.

Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Using unsaved module IDs, reversed connector directions, blocked clearance or an unbounded rule set.

## Migration Register

### Basic

#### 1. Step 1: Migration tracks replacement of a legacy visual with an approved Forge package while preserving the same gameplay identity.

Where you are: Migration Register / New source bar
What to click: Source type
What to select or type: Choose the source type named in the step (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Migration tracks replacement of a legacy visual with an approved Forge package while preserving the same gameplay identity.
Why now: This establishes the source and goal before dependent work begins.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The requested source type is visible and its relevant editor becomes available.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

## Recovery & History

### Basic

#### 1. Step 1: Use Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Y first for mistakes made during the current session.

Where you are: Recovery & History / New source bar
What to click: Source type
What to select or type: Choose the source type named in the step (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Use Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Y first for mistakes made during the current session. Painting batches, mode switches, face pastes, layer pastes and pattern applications are designed as undoable commands.
Why now: This establishes the source and goal before dependent work begins.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The requested source type is visible and its relevant editor becomes available.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 2. Step 2: Forge autosaves dirty work shortly after edits.

Where you are: Recovery & History / New source bar
What to click: Name
What to select or type: Training Example (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Forge autosaves dirty work shortly after edits. A recovery notice means a recoverable copy exists; it does not automatically replace the current source.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 3. Step 3: Review the candidate's asset ID, revision and time before opening it so you do not restore work from another asset.

Where you are: Recovery & History / New source bar
What to click: Stable slug
What to select or type: training_example (Lowercase letters, numbers and underscores; do not include the automatic domain prefix.)
Action: Review the candidate's asset ID, revision and time before opening it so you do not restore work from another asset.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 4. Step 4: Compare the recovered model and palette in Live Preview with the current saved source.

Where you are: Recovery & History / source editor
What to click: Preview output
What to select or type: Press the button after Name and Stable slug are valid (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Compare the recovered model and palette in Live Preview with the current saved source.
Why now: This uses the previous result and establishes a stable dependency for the next action.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

#### 5. Step 5: Restore only the intended candidate, then immediately Save Draft under the canonical source ID.

What to click: Save Draft
What to select or type: Use the concrete value stated in the action (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Restore only the intended candidate, then immediately Save Draft under the canonical source ID.

Where you are: Recovery & History / source editor
What to click: Validate
What to select or type: Press Validate; no text entry (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Run Validate after restoration because an autosave may have captured a structurally incomplete step.

What to click: Preview
What to select or type: Compare the preview with the expected result (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Use the previous-source backup when a completed save—not merely an unsaved edit—needs to be rolled back.

## Presentation Forge

### Basic

#### 1. Step 1: Use Presentation Forge after the block, item, entity or blueprint has a stable ID.

Where you are: Presentation Forge / New source bar
What to click: Source type
What to select or type: Choose the source type named in the step (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Use Presentation Forge after the block, item, entity or blueprint has a stable ID. It coordinates how gameplay events become bounded visual and audio responses without owning gameplay truth.

#### 1. Step 1: Select an accessibility profile and quality tier before running a scenario.

Where you are: Accessibility Preview / New source bar
What to click: Source type
What to select or type: Choose the source type named in the step (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Select an accessibility profile and quality tier before running a scenario. Test reduced motion, reduced flash, captions and critical-cue redundancy independently.
Why now: This establishes the source and goal before dependent work begins.
What this controls: This step changes the editable Forge source while preserving its stable gameplay identity.
Expected result: The requested source type is visible and its relevant editor becomes available.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: stable ID, source, runtime product, bake, receipt and approval.

What to click: Name

Expected result: The finished capstone reopens with a receipt-matched preview while human publication approval remains explicit.
How to verify: Reopen the source, run Validate again and confirm the preview and stable ID match the finished capstone.
Common mistakes: Changing a stable ID accidentally, editing a generated product, or approving before validation and preview.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.

What to select or type: Press the button after Name and Stable slug are valid (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Create a Rig. Bind root, head and limb roles, then add joints with parent/child roles, rotation axes, angle limits and motion profiles.
Why now: legal joint motion must be established before animation keys are authored.
What this controls: This edits reusable anatomy, semantic rig roles, motion or appearance data. Stable body roles let animations and equipment survive visual revisions.
Expected result: The source state and preview update to show the value or component described in this step.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Animating before role IDs are stable, binding the wrong body part, or duplicating anatomy for a cosmetic variant.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.
Related information: Glossary: source, rig, bake, receipt, approval. Related: VFX/audio event markers and Test & Delivery.

Action: Create a Form for visible volume, a Graph for bounded behaviour and an Effect to assemble them.

## Waveform & Event Editor

### Basic

#### 1. Step 1: Choose Synth Instrument / Sound for file-free creation, or Sound Source for an optional recorded file.

Where you are: Waveform & Event Editor / New source bar
What to click: Source type
What to select or type: Choose the source type named in the step (Use the visible field format and stable lowercase IDs where requested.)
Action: Choose Synth Instrument / Sound for file-free creation, or Sound Source for an optional recorded file.
Why now: This establishes the source and goal before dependent work begins.
What this controls: This edits a reproducible sound recipe, non-destructive source transform or managed Sound Event. Playback ownership and limits prevent stuck loops and excessive voices.
Expected result: The requested source type is visible and its relevant editor becomes available.
How to verify: Read the textual source-state line and diagnostics, then confirm the expected change is visible; colour alone is never the pass indicator.
Common mistakes: Clipping gain, an inaudible envelope, a loop without a stop path, missing caption, or too many simultaneous voices.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.

Common mistakes: Clipping gain, an inaudible envelope, a loop without a stop path, missing caption, or too many simultaneous voices.
How to recover: Press Ctrl+Z for an editable change, Retry after correcting the field, or Reset example to return to a new guide draft. The guide never overwrites the source you had open.

Action: Save Draft and Bake the Synth, Source, Spatial Profile and Event in that order; reopen the Event and play it twice with the same seed. Leave approval open until the Audio review batch accepts the audition.
Why now: the final receipt-matched chain proves every Audio Forge tool can produce and reproduce a usable sound without an uploaded file.
What this controls: This edits a reproducible sound recipe, non-destructive source transform or managed Sound Event. Playback ownership and limits prevent stuck loops and excessive voices.

How to verify: Reopen the source, run Validate again and confirm the preview and stable ID match the finished capstone.
Common mistakes: Clipping gain, an inaudible envelope, a loop without a stop path, missing caption, or too many simultaneous voices.

## Glossary

- **stable ID:** The permanent identity referenced by saves and other sources; changing the display name does not change it.
- **source:** The editable Forge document. It is canonical and must never be replaced by editing a generated product.
- **runtime product:** The replaceable result compiled or baked from a source for the game to load.
- **bake:** A deterministic conversion from editable source to a runtime product.
- **receipt:** Evidence binding the exact source and dependency hashes to one baked product.
- **connector:** A typed position, direction and clearance contract used to join structure modules safely.
- **rig:** Semantic joints and role bindings that tell body parts how they may move.
- **graph:** A bounded network of VFX operations whose connections define execution order.
- **spatial profile:** Reusable distance, attenuation, occlusion and reverb rules for a sound.
- **approval:** A human decision that a validated candidate is suitable to replace its fallback.
